operands' model of `Ord`: what `a >= b` calls.\npub def ge[o : Model(Ord)](a : o.Carrier, b : o.Carrier) -> Bool { o.ge a b }\n\n/// A positive number in binary, its least digit outermost, as Isabelle/HOL's\n/// `num`: one, or twice a positive, or twice a positive and one.\npub data Pos : Type where\n  /// One.\n  | One : Pos\n  /// Twice `p`.\n  | Bit0 : (p : Pos) --> Pos\n  /// Twice `p`, and one.\n  | Bit1 : (p : Pos) --> Pos\n\n/// A natural number in binary: zero, or a positive. A numeral annotated\n/// `(n : Nat)` is its constructor term.\npub data Nat : Type where\n  /// Zero.\n  | Zero : Nat\n  /// A positive number.\n  | Positive : (p : Pos) --> Nat\n\n/// A carrier a natural literal reads into: a literal checked where `Carrier`\n/// is expected is `fromNat` of its primitive integer.\npub theory FromNat where\n  sort Carrier : Type;\n  oper fromNat : (n : Integer) --> Carrier;\n\n/// A carrier a negative literal reads into: `-n` checked where `Carrier` is\n/// expected is `fromNeg` of the magnitude `n`, at the operand type `fromNat`\n/// takes.\npub theory FromNeg where\n  sort Carrier : Type;\n  oper fromNeg : (n : Integer) --> Carrier;\n\n/// A carrier a decimal literal reads into: a decimal checked where `Carrier`\n/// is expected is `fromDecimal` of its exact digits `mantissa` and its\n/// `exponent`, the value `mantissa × 10^exponent`, as Lean's `OfScientific`.\n/// A decimal at `f64` reads as the float it rounds to, through no model.\npub theory FromDecimal where\n  sort Carrier : Type;\n  oper fromDecimal : (mantissa : Integer, exponent : Integer) --> Carrier;\n\n//! The data subject's shared declarations, written once and imported by every\n//! source that reads them (`import \"./data-base.gandr\";`): the free naturals,\n//! their addition and the numerals two and three, the integers presented by two\n//! cancelling constructors, a three-colour enumeration, the two branches of a\n//! binary node and the polymorphic lists. A data block is an inductive type\n//! given by its constructors [MartinLof-1984-intuitionistic_type_theory], and a\n//! rule between constructor terms quotients it\n//! [Altenkirch-2018-quotient_inductive_inductive_types]. The universe subject\n//! reads the naturals and the branches from here too.\n//!\n//! - cites: [Altenkirch-2018-quotient_inductive_inductive_types]\n//! - cites: [MartinLof-1984-intuitionistic_type_theory]\n//!\n//! [Altenkirch-2018-quotient_inductive_inductive_types]: https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-89366-2_16 \"Altenkirch et al., Quotient inductive-inductive types\"\n//! [MartinLof-1984-intuitionistic_type_theory]: https://archive-pml.github.io/martin-lof/pdfs/Bibliopolis-Book-retypeset-1984.pdf \"Martin-Löf, Intuitionistic Type Theory\"\n\n/// Natural numbers, free: every constructor term is its own value.\npub data Nat : Type where\n  | Zero : Nat\n  | Succ : (n : Nat) --> Nat\n\n/// Addition by recursion on the second argument, the recursive call nested\n/// in the successor it returns.\npub def rec plus(n : Nat, m : Nat) -> Nat =\n  match m\n    | Zero => n\n    | Succ(p) => Succ (plus[<] n p)\n\n/// Two.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ Zero)\") ]\npub def two : Nat = Succ (Succ Zero);\n\n/// Three.\n@[ runs(\"Succ (Succ (Succ Zero))\") ]\npub def three : Nat = Succ two;\n\n/// The integers as a presented type: a successor and a predecessor that\n/// cancel, each rule invertible.\n@[ normalizes(\"S(P(S(Z))) ~> S(Z)\") ]\npub data Int : Type where {\n  | Z : Int\n  | S : (x : Int) --> Int\n  | P : (x : Int) --> Int\n  rule : S (P x) <=> x;\n  rule : P (S x) <=> x;\n}\n\n/// Three colours: an enumeration, a data type whose constructors take no\n/// field.\npub data Color : Type where\n  | Red : Color\n  | Green : Color\n  | Blue : Color\n\n/// The two branches of a binary node.\npub data Two : Type where\n  | L : Two\n  | R : Two\n\n/// Lists of elements of a type `a`. The element stands only where a list\n/// holds it, strictly positive, so a family nests through it.\npub data List(a : Type) : Type where\n  | Nil : List(a)\n  | Cons : (head : a, tail : List(a)) --> List(a)\n\n// `std.layout`: Rust's default layout as a program over codes. A type's code\n// lists its fields' scalars for a struct, or its variants' fields for an\n// enum. The program places them as rustc places a `repr(Rust)` type.\n//\n// `univariant` sorts a struct's fields by alignment, largest first. It moves\n// the niche toward the start, or toward the end when the start leaves it\n// nearer the middle. `enumLayout` builds two layouts, one filling a niche and\n// one with a tag. It keeps the smaller, then the one with the larger niche,\n// then the one with a tag. It widens a tag to the smallest first-field\n// alignment.\n//\n// rustc is the authority on a Rust type's layout. A corpus source checks the\n// program against what rustc prints for ten types.\n//\n// Three cases wait. A field that is itself an aggregate, an array or a\n// zero-sized type waits for the program to run over levitated codes. Its\n// alignment group follows from its size. A niche beyond `bool`'s and a\n// reference's needs a theory of the target: `char`'s, `NonZero`'s or an\n// enum's own. The program models no `repr` attribute and no target but\n// x86-64.\n//\n// - see: `compiler/rustc_abi/src/layout.rs` — rustc's `univariant`,\n//   `univariant_biased` and `layout_of_enum`\n// - see: `corpus/model/layouts/rust-default.gandr` — the program checked\n//   against rustc's ten layouts\n// - see: #2343\n\n/// A field's scalar type, its size its alignment on x86-64.\npub data Scalar : Type where\n  /// `u8`.\n  | U8 : Scalar\n  /// `u16`.\n  | U16 : Scalar\n  /// `u32`.\n  | U32 : Scalar\n  /// `u64`.\n  | U64 : Scalar\n  /// `i32`.\n  | I32 : Scalar\n  /// `i64`.\n  | I64 : Scalar\n  /// `bool`, whose 254 values past `true` are invalid.\n  | Flag : Scalar\n  /// `&T`, whose null is invalid.\n  | Ref : Scalar\n\n/// A struct's fields in declaration order.\npub data Fields : Type where\n  /// No more fields.\n  | Done : Fields\n  /// A named field, then the rest.\n  | Field : (name : String, ty : Scalar, rest : Fields) --> Fields\n\n/// An enum's variants in declaration order.\npub data Variants : Type where\n  /// No more variants.\n  | NoVariant : Variants\n  /// A named variant with its fields, then the rest.\n  | Variant : (name : String, fields : Fields, rest : Variants) --> Variants\n\n/// Fields in memory order, each at its offset.\npub data Placed : Type where\n  /// No more fields.\n  | End : Placed\n  /// A field at an offset, then the rest.\n  | At : (name : String, offset : Integer, rest : Placed) --> Placed\n\n/// A struct's layout as rustc prints it.\npub data Layout : Type where\n  /// Size, alignment and the placement of the fields.\n  | Layout : (size : Integer, align : Integer, fields : Placed) --> Layout\n\n/// Where an enum keeps which variant it holds.\npub data Discriminant : Type where\n  /// In the invalid values of the field at an offset.\n  | InNiche : (offset : Integer) --> Discriminant\n  /// In a tag of a size at offset zero.\n  | Tag : (size : Integer) --> Discriminant\n\n/// Each variant's placement, in declaration order.\npub data Arms : Type where\n  /// No more variants.\n  | NoArm : Arms\n  /// A variant's placement, then the rest.\n  | Arm : (name : String, fields : Placed, rest : Arms) --> Arms\n\n/// An enum's layout as rustc prints it.\npub data EnumLayout : Type where\n  /// Size, alignment, the discriminant and each variant's fields.\n  | EnumLayout : (\n    size : Integer,\n    align : Integer,\n    discriminant : Discriminant,\n    arms : Arms\n  ) --> EnumLayout\n\n/// A layout's largest niche.\ndata Niche : Type where\n  /// No field has invalid values.\n  | NoNiche : Niche\n  /// The field at an offset, its width, and how many invalid values it has.\n  | NicheAt : (offset : Integer, width : Integer, available : Integer) --> Niche\n\n/// A layout with its largest niche.\ndata Laid : Type where\n  /// Size, alignment, the fields placed and the largest niche.\n  | Laid : (size : Integer, align : Integer, fields : Placed, niche : Niche) --> Laid\n\n/// Which end of its alignment group a struct's niche sorts to.\ndata Bias : Type where\n  /// Larger niches first.\n  | NicheFirst : Bias\n  /// Larger niches last.\n  | NicheLast : Bias\n\n/// An order of fields.\ndata Order : Type where\n  /// A struct's: larger alignment first, then the niche toward the bias.\n  | InStruct : (bias : Bias) --> Order\n  /// A tagged variant's: smaller alignment first, then smaller niche.\n  | AfterTag : Order\n\n/// The layout of each variant.\ndata Laids : Type where\n  /// No more variants.\n  | NoLaid : Laids\n  /// A variant's layout, then the rest.\n  | OneLaid : (name : String, laid : Laid, rest : Laids) --> Laids\n\n/// A layout with how many invalid values it leaves, or none.\ndata Filled : Type where\n  /// No such layout.\n  | NoFill : Filled\n  /// The layout and its remaining niche.\n  | Filled : (layout : EnumLayout, remaining : Integer) --> Filled\n\n/// A placement of variants around a niche, or a variant that does not fit.\ndata Fitted : Type where\n  /// A variant fits neither before nor after the niche.\n  | Misfit : Fitted\n  /// The placement of every variant.\n  | Fitted : (arms : Arms) --> Fitted\n\n/// A scalar's size, which is its alignment.\ndef bytes(s : Scalar) -> Integer =\n  match s\n    | U8 => 1\n    | U16 => 2\n    | U32 => 4\n    | U64 => 8\n    | I32 => 4\n    | I64 => 8\n    | Flag => 1\n    | Ref => 8\n\n/// How many invalid values a scalar has.\ndef niche(s : Scalar) -> Integer =\n  match s\n    | U8 => 0\n    | U16 => 0\n    | U32 => 0\n    | U64 => 0\n    | I32 => 0\n    | I64 => 0\n    | Flag => 254\n    | Ref => 1\n\n/// The larger of two integers.\ndef bigger(a : Integer, b : Integer) -> Integer =\n  match a >= b\n    | true => a\n    | false => b\n\n/// The smaller of two integers.\ndef smaller(a : Integer, b : Integer) -> Integer =\n  match a >= b\n    | true => b\n    | false => a\n\n/// Whether two integers are equal.\ndef same(a : Integer, b : Integer) -> Bool =\n  match a >= b\n    | true => b >= a\n    | false => false\n\n/// One of two integers, by a condition.\ndef pick(c : Bool, yes : Integer, no : Integer) -> Integer =\n  match c\n    | true => yes\n    | false => no\n\n/// The first multiple of an alignment at or past an offset.\ndef up(o : Integer, a : Integer) -> Integer =\n  let q = int.div (o + a - 1) a\n  q * a\n\n/// Whether an order places `a` no later than `b`.\ndef before(o : Order, a : Scalar, b : Scalar) -> Bool =\n  let x = bytes a\n  let y = bytes b\n  let m = niche a\n  let n = niche b\n  match o\n    | InStruct(bias) => match x >= y\n      | false => false\n      | true => match y >= x\n        | false => true\n        | true => match bias\n          | NicheFirst => m >= n\n          | NicheLast => n >= m\n    | AfterTag => match y >= x\n      | false => false\n      | true => match x >= y\n        | false => true\n        | true => n >= m\n\n/// A field inserted into fields already in memory order, after every field it does not go before.\ndef rec insert(sorted : Fields, o : Order, n : String, s : Scalar) -> Fields =\n  match sorted\n    | Done => Field n s Done\n    | Field(m, t, rest) => {\n      let first = before o s t;\n      match first\n        | true => Field n s (Field m t rest)\n        | false => Field m t (insert[<] rest o n s)\n    }\n\n/// Fields in memory order, by a stable insertion sort, as rustc's `sort_by_key` is stable.\ndef rec order(fs : Fields, o : Order) -> Fields =\n  match fs\n    | Done => Done\n    | Field(n, s, rest) => insert (order[<] rest o) o n s\n\n/// How many fields there are.\ndef rec count(fs : Fields) -> Integer =\n  match fs\n    | Done => 0\n    | Field(n, s, rest) => {\n      let c = count[<] rest;\n      c + 1\n    }\n\n/// The placement of fields from an offset, each at the next multiple of its alignment.\ndef rec placeFrom(fs : Fields, o : Integer) -> Placed =\n  match fs\n    | Done => End\n    | Field(n, s, rest) => {\n      let b = bytes s;\n      let off = up o b;\n      At n off (placeFrom[<] rest (off + b))\n    }\n\n/// Where the last field ends, placed from an offset.\ndef rec endFrom(fs : Fields, o : Integer) -> Integer =\n  match fs\n    | Done => o\n    | Field(n, s, rest) => {\n      let b = bytes s;\n      let off = up o b;\n      endFrom[<] rest (off + b)\n    }\n\n/// The largest alignment, one when there is none.\ndef rec widest(fs : Fields) -> Integer =\n  match fs\n    | Done => 1\n    | Field(n, s, rest) => bigger (bytes s) (widest[<] rest)\n\n/// How many invalid values a niche has, none for no niche.\ndef room(n : Niche) -> Integer =\n  match n\n    | NoNiche => 0\n    | NicheAt(off, width, available) => available\n\n/// The largest niche of fields placed from an offset, the first in memory order among equals.\ndef rec nicheFrom(fs : Fields, o : Integer, best : Niche) -> Niche =\n  match fs\n    | Done => best\n    | Field(n, s, rest) => {\n      let b = bytes s;\n      let off = up o b;\n      let v = niche s;\n      let held = room best;\n      match held >= v\n        | true => nicheFrom[<] rest (off + b) best\n        | false => nicheFrom[<] rest (off + b) (NicheAt off b v)\n    }\n\n/// The layout of fields in memory order after a prefix of a size and an alignment.\ndef laidOut(sorted : Fields, prefix : Integer, prefixAlign : Integer) -> Laid =\n  let w = widest sorted\n  let a = bigger w prefixAlign\n  let e = endFrom sorted prefix\n  Laid (up e a) a (placeFrom sorted prefix) (nicheFrom sorted prefix NoNiche)\n\n/// The layout of a struct's fields under a bias, as `univariant_biased` lays them out.\ndef biased(fs : Fields, bias : Bias) -> Laid =\n  laidOut (order fs (InStruct bias)) 0 1\n\n/// The offset of a layout's niche, or zero when it has none.\ndef nicheOffset(l : Laid) -> Integer =\n  match l\n    | Laid(size, a, placed, n) => match n\n      | NoNiche => 0\n      | NicheAt(off, width, available) => off\n\n/// A struct's layout, as `univariant` chooses it: niches first, unless niches last puts the niche further from both edges' padding.\ndef univariant(fs : Fields) -> Laid =\n  let first = biased fs NicheFirst\n  let c = count fs\n  match first\n    | Laid(size, a, placed, n) => match n\n      | NoNiche => first\n      | NicheAt(head, width, available) => match c >= 2\n        | false => first\n        | true => match head >= 1\n          | false => first\n          | true => match size - head - width >= 1\n            | false => first\n            | true => {\n              let alt = biased fs NicheLast;\n              let altHead = nicheOffset alt;\n              match altHead >= head + 1\n                | false => first\n                | true => match altHead >= size - head - width + 1\n                  | false => first\n                  | true => alt\n            }\n\n/// A struct's layout: rustc's for a `repr(Rust)` struct of these fields.\npub def structLayout(fs : Fields) -> Layout =\n  let l = univariant fs\n  match l\n    | Laid(size, a, placed, n) => Layout size a placed\n\n/// The layout of each variant as a struct.\ndef rec asStructs(vs : Variants) -> Laids =\n  match vs\n    | NoVariant => NoLaid\n    | Variant(n, fs, rest) => OneLaid n (univariant fs) (asStructs[<] rest)\n\n/// The layout of each variant after a one-byte tag.\ndef rec afterTag(vs : Variants) -> Laids =\n  match vs\n    | NoVariant => NoLaid\n    | Variant(n, fs, rest) => {\n      let sorted = order fs AfterTag;\n      OneLaid n (laidOut sorted 1 1) (afterTag[<] rest)\n    }\n\n/// How many variants there are.\ndef rec variantCount(ls : Laids) -> Integer =\n  match ls\n    | NoLaid => 0\n    | OneLaid(n, l, rest) => {\n      let c = variantCount[<] rest;\n      c + 1\n    }\n\n/// The largest alignment of the variants, one when there is none.\ndef rec widestOf(ls : Laids) -> Integer =\n  match ls\n    | NoLaid => 1\n    | OneLaid(n, l, rest) => {\n      let w = widestOf[<] rest;\n      match l\n        | Laid(size, a, placed, nn) => bigger a w\n    }\n\n/// The largest size of the variants, zero when there is none.\ndef rec largestSize(ls : Laids) -> Integer =\n  match ls\n    | NoLaid => 0\n    | OneLaid(n, l, rest) => {\n      let w = largestSize[<] rest;\n      match l\n        | Laid(size, a, placed, nn) => bigger size w\n    }\n\n/// The index of the last largest variant, counting from `i`, as `max_by_key` keeps the last.\ndef rec largestFrom(ls : Laids, i : Integer, bestIndex : Integer, bestSize : Integer) -> Integer =\n  match ls\n    | NoLaid => bestIndex\n    | OneLaid(n, l, rest) => match l\n      | Laid(size, a, placed, nn) => match size >= bestSize\n        | true => largestFrom[<] rest (i + 1) i size\n        | false => largestFrom[<] rest (i + 1) bestIndex bestSize\n\n/// The variant at an index, where the count starts at `i`, or an empty layout past the end.\ndef rec laidAt(ls : Laids, i : Integer, want : Integer) -> Laid =\n  match ls\n    | NoLaid => Laid 0 1 End NoNiche\n    | OneLaid(n, l, rest) => {\n      let found = same i want;\n      match found\n        | true => l\n        | false => laidAt[<] rest (i + 1) want\n    }\n\n/// Each field's place plus an offset.\ndef rec shifted(p : Placed, by : Integer) -> Placed =\n  match p\n    | End => End\n    | At(n, off, rest) => {\n      let tail = shifted[<] rest by;\n      At n (off + by) tail\n    }\n\n/// Every variant but the largest placed before the niche or after it.\ndef rec around(\n  ls : Laids,\n  i : Integer,\n  largest : Integer,\n  off : Integer,\n  width : Integer,\n  size : Integer\n) -> Fitted =\n  match ls\n    | NoLaid => Fitted NoArm\n    | OneLaid(n, l, rest) => {\n      let tail = around[<] rest (i + 1) largest off width size;\n      let isLargest = same i largest;\n      match tail\n        | Misfit => Misfit\n        | Fitted(arms) => match l\n          | Laid(s, a, placed, nn) => match isLargest\n            | true => Fitted (Arm n placed arms)\n            | false => match off >= s\n              | true => Fitted (Arm n placed arms)\n              | false => {\n                let after = up (off + width) a;\n                match size >= after + s\n                  | true => Fitted (Arm n (shifted placed after) arms)\n                  | false => Misfit\n              }\n    }\n\n/// The niche-filled layout: the largest variant's niche holds which of the variants around it is there.\ndef nicheFilled(ls : Laids) -> Filled =\n  let n = variantCount ls\n  match n >= 2\n    | false => NoFill\n    | true => {\n      let big = largestFrom ls 0 0 (0 - 1);\n      let l = laidAt ls 0 big;\n      let a = widestOf ls;\n      let atStart = same big 0;\n      let atLast = same big (n - 1);\n      let firstNeed = pick atStart 1 0;\n      let lastNeed = pick atLast (n - 2) (n - 1);\n      let needed = lastNeed - firstNeed + 1;\n      match l\n        | Laid(s, la, placed, nn) => match nn\n          | NoNiche => NoFill\n          | NicheAt(off, width, available) => match available >= needed\n            | false => NoFill\n            | true => {\n              let size = up s a;\n              let fitted = around ls 0 big off width size;\n              match fitted\n                | Misfit => NoFill\n                | Fitted(arms) => Filled (EnumLayout size a (InNiche off) arms) (available - needed)\n            }\n    }\n\n/// The smallest alignment of a variant's first field after the tag, 256 when no variant has a field.\ndef rec firstAlign(ls : Laids) -> Integer =\n  match ls\n    | NoLaid => 256\n    | OneLaid(n, l, rest) => {\n      let r = firstAlign[<] rest;\n      match l\n        | Laid(size, a, placed, nn) => match placed\n          | End => r\n          | At(m, off, t) => smaller a r\n    }\n\n/// The placement of the variants' fields.\ndef rec armsOf(ls : Laids) -> Arms =\n  match ls\n    | NoLaid => NoArm\n    | OneLaid(n, l, rest) => {\n      let t = armsOf[<] rest;\n      match l\n        | Laid(size, a, placed, nn) => Arm n placed t\n    }\n\n/// How many values a tag of a size holds.\ndef tagRange(t : Integer) -> Integer =\n  match t >= 8\n    | true => 18446744073709551616\n    | false => match t >= 4\n      | true => 4294967296\n      | false => match t >= 2\n        | true => 65536\n        | false => 256\n\n/// The tagged layout, whose tag widens to the smallest first-field alignment.\ndef taggedFill(vs : Variants) -> Filled =\n  let ls = afterTag vs\n  let n = variantCount ls\n  let a = widestOf ls\n  let s = largestSize ls\n  let size = up s a\n  let start = firstAlign ls\n  let fits = 8 >= start\n  let tag = pick fits start 1\n  let arms = armsOf ls\n  let range = tagRange tag\n  Filled (EnumLayout size a (Tag tag) arms) (range - n)\n\n/// An enum's size.\ndef enumSize(l : EnumLayout) -> Integer =\n  match l\n    | EnumLayout(size, a, d, arms) => size\n\n/// An enum's layout: rustc's for a `repr(Rust)` enum of these variants.\npub def enumLayout(vs : Variants) -> EnumLayout =\n  let ls = asStructs vs\n  let nl = nicheFilled ls\n  let tl = taggedFill vs\n  match tl\n    | NoFill => EnumLayout 0 1 (Tag 0) NoArm\n    | Filled(tlay, tniche) => match nl\n      | NoFill => tlay\n      | Filled(nlay, nniche) => {\n        let ts = enumSize tlay;\n        let ns = enumSize nlay;\n        match ts >= ns + 1\n          | true => nlay\n          | false => match ns >= ts + 1\n            | true => tlay\n            | false => match nniche >= tniche + 1\n              | true => nlay\n              | false => tlay\n      }\n\n// `std.boundary`: a function row of the foreign boundary and its admission. A\n// row lists its arguments' codes and its result's.\n//\n// `summary` reads a code's witness record off Rust's default layout,\n// `std.layout`: its size, stride, alignment and handling.\n// `admit(declared, row)` compares a declared signature's printed layouts with\n// the records clause by clause. It reads the arity, then each argument's size,\n// alignment, niche and handling, then the result's. It answers `Admitted`, or\n// `Unrealised` with the first clause that fails.\n//\n// The checker holds the layout program's integer comparisons as neutrals, so\n// admission is a run. A corpus source admits Rust externs against a row.\n//\n// - see: `corpus/model/interop/boundary/function-row.gandr` — Rust externs\n//   against a row\n\n// `Scalar`, `Fields`, `Variants`, `structLayout`, `enumLayout`.\nimport \"./layout.gandr\";\n\n/// A type at the row, as Rust's default layout codes it.\npub data Code : Type where\n  /// A scalar.\n  | Plain : (s : Scalar) --> Code\n  /// An enum.\n  | Sum : (vs : Variants) --> Code\n\n/// How a program copies, moves, destroys and relocates a value.\npub data Handling : Type where\n  /// As its bytes: copied, moved and relocated by copying them, destroyed by\n  /// nothing.\n  | Bitwise : Handling\n\n/// The witness record: size, stride, alignment, and the value's handling.\npub data Witness : Type where\n  /// The record.\n  | Witness : (size : Integer, stride : Integer, align : Integer, handling : Handling) --> Witness\n\n/// The first multiple of an alignment at or past a size.\ndef strideOf(size : Integer, align : Integer) -> Integer =\n  let q = int.div (size + align - 1) align\n  q * align\n\n/// The record a code's layout gives. Every code here is a scalar, a reference\n/// or an enum of them, so every value travels bitwise.\npub def summary(c : Code) -> Witness =\n  match c\n    | Plain(s) => match structLayout (Field \"0\" s Done)\n      | Layout(size, align, _p) => Witness size (strideOf size align) align Bitwise\n    | Sum(vs) => match enumLayout vs\n      | EnumLayout(size, align, _d, _a) => Witness size (strideOf size align) align Bitwise\n\n/// Whether a code's layout keeps its discriminant in a niche.\npub def niched(c : Code) -> Bool =\n  match c\n    | Plain(_s) => false\n    | Sum(vs) => match enumLayout vs\n      | EnumLayout(_size, _align, d, _a) => match d\n        | InNiche(_o) => true\n        | _ => false\n\n/// Argument codes in order.\npub data Codes : Type where\n  /// No more arguments.\n  | NoCode : Codes\n  /// An argument's code, then the rest.\n  | Arg : (c : Code, rest : Codes) --> Codes\n\n/// A function row: its arguments' codes and its result's.\npub data Row : Type where\n  /// The row.\n  | Row : (args : Codes, result : Code) --> Row\n\n/// A type's layout as rustc prints it: size, alignment, a niche, and whether\n/// it needs drop.\npub data Printed : Type where\n  /// The printed layout.\n  | Printed : (size : Integer, align : Integer, niched : Bool, drops : Bool) --> Printed\n\n/// An extern's parameters in order, each named, as rustc builds them.\npub data Params : Type where\n  /// No more parameters.\n  | NoParam : Params\n  /// A parameter's name and printed layout, then the rest.\n  | Param : (name : String, p : Printed, rest : Params) --> Params\n\n/// An extern's declared signature: its parameters and its result.\npub data Declared : Type where\n  /// The signature.\n  | Declared : (params : Params, result : Printed) --> Declared\n\n/// Where a clause stands in the row.\npub data Position : Type where\n  /// The argument at an index, from zero.\n  | Argument : (index : Integer) --> Position\n  /// The result.\n  | Result : Position\n\n/// The clause of the row a declared signature breaks.\npub data Clause : Type where\n  /// The extern takes a different number of arguments.\n  | Arity : Clause\n  /// The block declares a different number of members than the attribute\n  /// names rows.\n  | Members : Clause\n  /// A size differs.\n  | Size : (place : Position) --> Clause\n  /// An alignment differs.\n  | Align : (place : Position) --> Clause\n  /// One keeps its discriminant in a niche and the other does not.\n  | Niche : (place : Position) --> Clause\n  /// The declared type needs drop, and the row's value travels bitwise.\n  | Destroy : (place : Position) --> Clause\n\n/// What admission answers.\npub data Admission : Type where\n  /// The declared signature realises the row.\n  | Admitted : Admission\n  /// It does not, by the first clause that fails.\n  | Unrealised : (c : Clause) --> Admission\n\n/// Whether two booleans are equal.\ndef agree(a : Bool, b : Bool) -> Bool =\n  match a\n    | true => b\n    | false => match b\n      | true => false\n      | false => true\n\n/// One position of the row: the printed layout against the code's record,\n/// size, then alignment, then the niche, then the handling.\npub def check(p : Printed, c : Code, place : Position) -> Admission =\n  match p\n    | Printed(size, align, inNiche, drops) => match summary c\n      | Witness(s, _stride, a, h) => match size == s\n        | false => Unrealised (Size place)\n        | true => match align == a\n          | false => Unrealised (Align place)\n          | true => match agree inNiche (niched c)\n            | false => Unrealised (Niche place)\n            | true => match h\n              | Bitwise => match drops\n                | true => Unrealised (Destroy place)\n                | false => Admitted\n\n/// The arguments from an index, in order, as many on each side.\ndef rec admitArgs(ps : Params, cs : Codes, i : Integer) -> Admission =\n  match ps\n    | NoParam => match cs\n      | NoCode => Admitted\n      | Arg(_c, _rest) => Unrealised Arity\n    | Param(_n, p, rest) => match cs\n      | NoCode => Unrealised Arity\n      | Arg(c, more) => match check p c (Argument i)\n        | Admitted => admitArgs[<] rest more (i + 1)\n        | Unrealised(cl) => Unrealised cl\n\n/// A declared signature against a row: the arguments, then the result.\npub def admit(d : Declared, r : Row) -> Admission =\n  match d\n    | Declared(ps, res) => match r\n      | Row(cs, rc) => match admitArgs ps cs 0\n        | Admitted => check res rc Result\n        | Unrealised(cl) => Unrealised cl\n\n/// An extern's parameters in order, each named and coded as its declared type\n/// is.\npub data Coded : Type where\n  /// No more parameters.\n  | NoCoded : Coded\n  /// A parameter's name and its type's code, then the rest.\n  | Coded : (name : String, c : Code, rest : Coded) --> Coded\n\n/// An extern's signature as its declared types' codes: what an `extern \"rust\"`\n/// block declares, before Rust's layout prints it.\npub data Signature : Type where\n  /// The signature.\n  | Signature : (params : Coded, result : Code) --> Signature\n\n/// A code's layout as rustc prints it, from Rust's default layout. Every code\n/// here travels bitwise, so none needs drop.\npub def printed(c : Code) -> Printed =\n  match summary c\n    | Witness(size, _stride, align, _h) => Printed size align (niched c) false\n\n/// The printed layout of each parameter's code, in order.\ndef rec printedParams(cs : Coded) -> Params =\n  match cs\n    | NoCoded => NoParam\n    | Coded(n, c, rest) => Param n (printed c) (printedParams[<] rest)\n\n/// A signature against a row: Rust's default layout prints the type of each\n/// declaration, and admission compares it with the row.\npub def admitSignature(s : Signature, r : Row) -> Admission =\n  match s\n    | Signature(ps, res) => admit (Declared (printedParams ps) (printed res)) r\n\n/// An `extern \"rust\"` block's members' signatures, in written order.\npub data Signatures : Type where\n  /// No more members.\n  | NoSignature : Signatures\n  /// A member's signature, then the rest.\n  | Member : (s : Signature, rest : Signatures) --> Signatures\n\n/// The rows a `boundary` attribute names, one per member in written order.\npub data Rows : Type where\n  /// No more rows.\n  | NoRow : Rows\n  /// A row, then the rest.\n  | Named : (r : Row, rest : Rows) --> Rows\n\n/// Each member against its row, in order, until one fails: the answer names\n/// the first unrealised member. A block of more members than rows, or fewer,\n/// answers `Unrealised` at `Members`. The check of a block carrying\n/// `@[ boundary(…) ]` runs it.\npub def rec admitBlock(ss : Signatures, rs : Rows) -> Admission =\n  match ss\n    | NoSignature => match rs\n      | NoRow => Admitted\n      | Named(_r, _rest) => Unrealised Members\n    | Member(s, more) => match rs\n      | NoRow => Unrealised Members\n      | Named(r, rest) => match admitSignature s r\n        | Admitted => admitBlock[<] more rest\n        | Unrealised(cl) => Unrealised cl\n\n//! A data type with one parameter: an optional value, absent or present. A\n//! match on a constructor written with its type takes the arm that constructor\n//! names [MartinLof-1984-intuitionistic_type_theory].\n//!\n//! - see: #616 — `kept`\n//! - see: #1855 — `keptTally`, `keptDuo`\n//! - cites: [MartinLof-1984-intuitionistic_type_theory]\n//!\n//! [MartinLof-1984-intuitionistic_type_theory]: https://archive-pml.github.io/martin-lof/pdfs/Bibliopolis-Book-retypeset-1984.pdf \"Martin-Löf, Intuitionistic Type Theory\"\n\n/// An optional `a`: none, or some value.\npub data Maybe(a : Type) : Type where\n  | None : Maybe(a)\n  | Some : (x : a) --> Maybe(a)\n\n/// The value inside a present option, or zero for an absent one.\ndef unwrap_or_zero() -> Integer =\n  match (Some 3 : Maybe(Integer))\n    | None => 0\n    | Some(x) => x\n\n/// A match over a family of parameters whose answer names its scrutinee:\n/// each arm answers the type as written, `m` read as itself.\ndef kept(m : Maybe(Integer)) -> Path(m, m) =\n  match m\n    | None => Path.here m\n    | Some(x) => Path.here m\n\n/// One integer, its constructor named for its type.\ndata Tally : Type where\n  | Tally : (count : Integer) --> Tally\n\n/// Two types side by side, its constructor named for its type.\ndata Duo(a : Type, b : Type) : Type where\n  | Duo : (first : a, second : b) --> Duo(a, b)\n\n/// A tally kept as present: `Tally` at `Maybe`'s type parameter names the\n/// type, not the constructor that shares its name.\ndef keptTally(t : Tally) -> Maybe(Tally) =\n  (Some t : Maybe(Tally))\n\n/// A duo of tallies kept as present: `Duo(Tally, Tally)` at the same place\n/// is the type applied, and each `Tally` in it the type again.\ndef keptDuo(d : Duo(Tally, Tally)) -> Maybe(Duo(Tally, Tally)) =\n  (Some d : Maybe(Duo(Tally, Tally)))\n\n//! The null niche as a realizability predicate. A type's realisers are a\n//! predicate on a word and a heap, `V⟦T⟧ : (Nat, Heap) => Type`; the heap is\n//! data, the separating conjunction a sigma over a disjoint split and points-to\n//! a singleton, so the normaliser computes every assertion and the kernel\n//! replays every declaration here\n//! [Wagner-2024-realistic_realizability_specifying_abis_you_can_count], § 4.2.\n//! `Option<&T>` is one word, null or a reference; its realisers are equivalent\n//! at every heap to those of the two-word tagged layout, and the marshallers\n//! between the two meet their triples. A C function specified by hand,\n//! `deref_or`, is met by the niche; its `_Nonnull` neighbour is refused, in\n//! `../../pathological/specification/niche-realizability.gandr`.\n//!\n//! Words are `Nat`, null `Zero`, so a case on a word reduces; a triple is\n//! `P(h) => wp(c, Q, h)`, and a pure marshaller's precondition is its\n//! postcondition at its answer. Departing from § 4.2: both layouts are words\n//! by value rather than objects behind a reference count, so there is no\n//! `size` and no count; there is no later modality; and a triple is stated at\n//! the heap it owns, with no frame rule over it. Where a realiser runs to\n//! `emp(h)` the proofs case on the heap.\n//!\n//! - see: #2832\n//! - see: #327 — `derefOrWp`\n//! - cites: [Wagner-2024-realistic_realizability_specifying_abis_you_can_count]\n//!\n//! [Wagner-2024-realistic_realizability_specifying_abis_you_can_count]: https://doi.org/10.1145/3689755 \"Wagner, Eisbach and Ahmed, Realistic Realizability: Specifying ABIs You Can Count On\"\n\n// `Nat`.\nimport \"../data/data-base.gandr\";\n\n// `Maybe`.\nimport \"../data/data-maybe.gandr\";\n\n/// A heap: finitely many cells, each a location and the word it holds.\npub data Heap : Type where\n  | Emp : Heap\n  | Cell : (l : Nat, v : Nat, rest : Heap) --> Heap\n\n/// `yes` when the two words are one, `no` otherwise.\ndef rec pick(a : Nat, b : Nat, yes : Maybe(Nat), no : Maybe(Nat)) -> Maybe(Nat) =\n  match a\n    | Zero => match b\n      | Zero => yes\n      | Succ(_q) => no\n    | Succ(p) => match b\n      | Zero => no\n      | Succ(q) => pick[<] p q yes no\n\n/// The word a heap holds at a location, the first cell naming it.\ndef rec lookup(h : Heap, x : Nat) -> Maybe(Nat) =\n  match h\n    | Emp => None\n    | Cell(l, v, rest) => pick l x (Some v) (lookup[<] rest x)\n\n/// The empty heap.\npub def emp(h : Heap) -> Type =\n  match h\n    | Emp => return Unit\n    | Cell(_l, _v, _rest) => return Void\n\n/// The singleton heap holding `v` at `l`.\npub def pointsTo(l : Nat, v : Nat, h : Heap) -> Type =\n  match h\n    | Emp => return Void\n    | Cell(m, w, rest) => return type(*{\n      loc : Path[.A = Nat](m, l),\n      held : Path[.A = Nat](w, v),\n      only : emp(rest)\n    })\n\n/// One location read through a disjoint union: nothing on the left, and the\n/// union holds what the right part does; or a word on the left, the right\n/// part holding nothing there.\ndef union(o : Maybe(Nat), o1 : Maybe(Nat), o2 : Maybe(Nat)) -> Type =\n  match o1\n    | None => return Path o o2\n    | Some(v) => return type(*{\n      free : Path[.A = Maybe(Nat)](o2, (None : Maybe(Nat))),\n      held : Path[.A = Maybe(Nat)](o, (Some v : Maybe(Nat)))\n    })\n\n/// The separating conjunction: a split of the heap into two disjoint parts,\n/// the first satisfying `p` and the second `q`.\npub def star(p : Heap -> Type, q : Heap -> Type, h : Heap) -> Type =\n  return type(*{\n    h1 : Heap,\n    h2 : Heap,\n    split : (x : Nat) -> union(lookup(h, x), lookup(h1, x), lookup(h2, x)),\n    left : p(h1),\n    right : q(h2)\n  })\n\n/// The singleton heap at `l` holding `v`, as a predicate on heaps.\npub def pointsToAt(l : Nat, v : Nat) -> Heap -> Type =\n  return fun (h) { pointsTo l v h }\n\n/// `T`'s realisers at the word `v`, as a predicate on heaps.\npub def realisedAt(vt : (Nat, Heap) -> Type, v : Nat) -> Heap -> Type =\n  return fun (h) { vt v h }\n\n/// A reference to a `T`: a non-null word whose cell holds a realiser of `T`.\npub def vRef(vt : (Nat, Heap) -> Type, w : Nat, h : Heap) -> Type =\n  match w\n    | Zero => return Void\n    | Succ(_n) => return type(*{v : Nat, cell : star(pointsToAt(w, v), realisedAt(vt, v), h)})\n\n/// The null niche: null, owning nothing, or a reference.\npub def vOpt(vt : (Nat, Heap) -> Type, w : Nat, h : Heap) -> Type =\n  match w\n    | Zero => emp h\n    | Succ(_n) => vRef vt w h\n\n/// Two words: a tag and a payload.\npub data Tagged : Type where\n  | Tag : (t : Nat, p : }\n      | Succ(_n) => return fun (q) { q }\n    | Succ(_n) => return fun (none) { match (none : Void) {} }\n\n/// `toOptMeets` at a written pair, by its tag.\ndef toOptAt(vt : (Nat, Heap) -> Type, t niche realiser marshalled: the pair `fromOpt` answers, with the\n/// evidence `fromOptMeets` carries to it.\ndef marshalled(vt : (Nat, Heap) -> Type, h : Heap, w : Nat) -> (vOpt(vt, w, h)) -> *{\n  r : Tagged,\n  ok : vTag(vt, r, h)\n} =\n  match w\n    | Zero => match h\n      | Emp => return fun (ok) {\n        let meets = fromOptMeets vt Zero Emp;\n        let e = meets ok;\n        return
